Carries OCM-3 (13-band ocean colour with a very wide swath of over 1,000 km), SSTM (thermal sea-surface temperature), SCAT-3 (Ku-band scatterometer for ocean-surface wind) and ARGOS (data collection). Gives about 2-day coverage. Best for chlorophyll, algal blooms, fishery advisories, cyclone winds and sea-surface temperature. Limits: coarse over land. | Together: Oceansat-3 supplies ocean colour, sea-surface temperature and wind with near-polar coverage, filling the gap that geostationary INSAT satellites leave at the poles and over detailed ocean parameters. SCATSAT-1 (if active) adds extra wind vectors.

## Recommendation case 329: Meteorological / Global / GEO / Continuous
- Application: Meteorological
- Coverage: Global
- Requested orbit: GEO
- Requested revisit: Continuous
- Resolved orbit: GEO
- Feasibility: P (partial)
- Recommended ISRO satellites: INSAT-3DS, INSAT-3DR, INSAT-3D
- Best achievable revisit (hours): 0.5
- Revisit fit: Partially meets
- Rationale: Only the Indian Ocean Region full disk (about one-third of the globe). Other sectors need non-ISRO geostationary data.
- Alternative if unavailable: 
- Combination members: INSAT-3D family (3DS, 3DR, 3D)
- Combination details: INSAT-3D family (3DS, 3DR, 3D): INSAT-3D (Jul 2013), INSAT-3DR (Sep 2016, 74 E) and INSAT-3DS (Feb 2024, GSLV-F14) are ISRO's geostationary weather satellites at about 74 E to 82 E. Each has a 6-channel Imager (visible and shortwave infrared at about 1 km, mid and thermal infrared at about 4 km, water vapour at about 8 km); 3D and 3DR also carry a 19-channel Sounder for temperature and humidity profiles, plus data-relay and search-and-rescue payloads. Full disk about every 30 minutes with faster sector scans. Limits: cannot see the poles, coarse resolution. | Together: INSAT-3DS is the primary imager; INSAT-3DR and INSAT-3D at 74 E and 82 E give backup, cross-checks of cloud winds and extra sounder profiles. Together they deliver continuous cyclone, monsoon, fog and rainfall monitoring of the Indian Ocean Region.

## Recommendation case 341: Meteorological / Regional / LEO / Continuous
- Application: Meteorological
- Coverage: Regional
- Requested orbit: LEO
- Requested revisit: Continuous
- Resolved orbit: LEO
- Feasibility: N (native)
- Recommended ISRO satellites: Oceansat-3 (SSO), EOS-08
- Best achievable revisit (hours): 48.0
- Revisit fit: Does not meet
- Rationale: Tropical/sub-tropical Indian Ocean region is well covered by the inclined EOS-08 and polar Oceansat-3.
- Alternative if unavailable: 
- Combination members: Oceansat-3 (EOS-06) + EOS-08
- Combination details: Oceansat-3 (EOS-06): Launched 26 Nov 2022 on PSLV-C54, SSO about 720 km. Carries OCM-3 (13-band ocean colour with a very wide swath of over 1,000 km), SSTM (thermal sea-surface temperature), SCAT-3 (Ku-band scatterometer for ocean-surface wind) and ARGOS (data collection). Gives about 2-day coverage. Best for chlorophyll, algal blooms, fishery advisories, cyclone winds and sea-surface temperature. Limits: coarse over land. | EOS-08: Launched 16 Aug 2024 on SSLV-D3; a 175 kg microsatellite in a 475 km orbit inclined about 37 degrees. Payloads: EOIR (visible, mid-wave and long-wave infrared for day and night imaging, fire and volcano monitoring), GNSS-R (ocean wind, soil moisture, flood inundation) and an ultraviolet dosimeter supporting Gaganyaan. Limits: small satellite, restricted to about +/-40 degrees latitude. | Together: Polar Oceansat-3 and the tropical, inclined EOS-08 sample winds, sea state, sea-surface temperature and soil moisture at different times and latitudes, improving coverage over India and the Indian Ocean. EOS-08 is not used for Global or Polar requests.

## Recommendation case 353: Meteorological / Regional / Sun-synchronous / Continuous
- Application: Meteorological
- Coverage: Regional
- Requested orbit: Sun-synchronous
- Requested revisit: Continuous
- Resolved orbit: Sun-synchronous
- Feasibility: N (native)
- Recommended ISRO satellites: Oceansat-3 / EOS-06
- Best achievable revisit (hours): 48.0
- Revisit fit: Does not meet
- Rationale: Indian Ocean and neighbouring seas; ocean winds and SST feed cyclone and monsoon analysis.
- Alternative if unavailable: 
- Combination members: Oceansat-3 (EOS-06)
- Combination details: Oceansat-3 (EOS-06): Launched 26 Nov 2022 on PSLV-C54, SSO about 720 km. Carries OCM-3 (13-band ocean colour with a very wide swath of over 1,000 km), SSTM (thermal sea-surface temperature), SCAT-3 (Ku-band scatterometer for ocean-surface wind) and ARGOS (data collection). Gives about 2-day coverage. Best for chlorophyll, algal blooms, fishery advisories, cyclone winds and sea-surface temperature. Limits: coarse over land. | Together: Oceansat-3 supplies ocean colour, sea-surface temperature and wind with near-polar coverage, filling the gap that geostationary INSAT satellites leave at the poles and over detailed ocean parameters. SCATSAT-1 (if active) adds extra wind vectors.

## Recommendation case 361: Meteorological / National (India) / LEO / Continuous
- Application: Meteorological
- Coverage: National (India)
- Requested orbit: LEO
- Requested revisit: Continuous
- Resolved orbit: LEO
- Feasibility: N (native)
- Recommended ISRO satellites: Oceansat-3 (SSO) plus EOS-08 (EOIR + GNSS-R, 475 km, ~37 deg incl.)
- Best achievable revisit (hours): 24.0
- Revisit fit: Does not meet
- Rationale: EOS-08's GNSS-R measures ocean-surface wind and soil moisture/flood inundation; combined with Oceansat-3 for better sampling of India.
- Alternative if unavailable: 
- Combination members: Oceansat-3 (EOS-06) + EOS-08
- Combination details: Oceansat-3 (EOS-06): Launched 26 Nov 2022 on PSLV-C54, SSO about 720 km. Carries OCM-3 (13-band ocean colour with a very wide swath of over 1,000 km), SSTM (thermal sea-surface temperature), SCAT-3 (Ku-band scatterometer for ocean-surface wind) and ARGOS (data collection). Gives about 2-day coverage. Best for chlorophyll, algal blooms, fishery advisories, cyclone winds and sea-surface temperature. Limits: coarse over land. | EOS-08: Launched 16 Aug 2024 on SSLV-D3; a 175 kg microsatellite in a 475 km orbit inclined about 37 degrees. Payloads: EOIR (visible, mid-wave and long-wave infrared for day and night imaging, fire and volcano monitoring), GNSS-R (ocean wind, soil moisture, flood inundation) and an ultraviolet dosimeter supporting Gaganyaan. Limits: small satellite, restricted to about +/-40 degrees latitude. | Together: Polar Oceansat-3 and the tropical, inclined EOS-08 sample winds, sea state, sea-surface temperature and soil moisture at different times and latitudes, improving coverage over India and the Indian Ocean. EOS-08 is not used for Global or Polar requests.

## Recommendation case 373: Meteorological / National (India) / Sun-synchronous / Continuous
- Application: Meteorological
- Coverage: National (India)
- Requested orbit: Sun-synchronous
- Requested revisit: Continuous
- Resolved orbit: Sun-synchronous
- Feasibility: N (native)
- Recommended ISRO satellites: Oceansat-3 / EOS-06 (OCM-3, SSTM, SCAT-3 scatterometer, ARGOS); SCATSAT-1 legacy winds (verify status)
- Best achievable revisit (hours): 48.0
- Revisit fit: Does not meet
- Rationale: ~720 km SSO; ocean colour, sea-surface temperature, ocean-surface winds, chlorophyll, potential fishing zones; ~2-day coverage.
- Alternative if unavailable: 
- Combination members: Oceansat-3 (EOS-06) + SCATSAT-1
- Combination details: Oceansat-3 (EOS-06): Launched 26 Nov 2022 on PSLV-C54, SSO about 720 km. Carries OCM-3 (13-band ocean colour with a very wide swath of over 1,000 km), SSTM (thermal sea-surface temperature), SCAT-3 (Ku-band scatterometer for ocean-surface wind) and ARGOS (data collection). Gives about 2-day coverage. Best for chlorophyll, algal blooms, fishery advisories, cyclone winds and sea-surface temperature. Limits: coarse over land. | SCATSAT-1: Launched 26 Sep 2016 on PSLV-C35, SSO about 720 km. Ku-band pencil-beam scatterometer measuring ocean-surface wind speed and direction, used for cyclone detection and forecasting. Verify current operational status before use; its archived data remain valuable. | Together: Oceansat-3 supplies ocean colour, sea-surface temperature and wind with near-polar coverage, filling the gap that geostationary INSAT satellites leave at the poles and over detailed ocean parameters. SCATSAT-1 (if active) adds extra wind vectors.

## Recommendation case 381: Meteorological / Polar regions / LEO / Continuous
- Application: Meteorological
- Coverage: Polar regions
- Requested orbit: LEO
- Requested revisit: Continuous
- Resolved orbit: LEO
- Feasibility: N (native)
- Recommended ISRO satellites: Oceansat-3 (SSO subset of LEO)
- Best achievable revisit (hours): 12.0
- Revisit fit: Does not meet
- Rationale: Only the near-polar SSO satellite reaches the poles; inclined LEO satellites cannot.
- Alternative if unavailable: 
- Combination members: Oceansat-3 (EOS-06)
- Combination details: Oceansat-3 (EOS-06): Launched 26 Nov 2022 on PSLV-C54, SSO about 720 km. Carries OCM-3 (13-band ocean colour with a very wide swath of over 1,000 km), SSTM (thermal sea-surface temperature), SCAT-3 (Ku-band scatterometer for ocean-surface wind) and ARGOS (data collection). Gives about 2-day coverage. Best for chlorophyll, algal blooms, fishery advisories, cyclone winds and sea-surface temperature.
